# Jolt WebGPU Prover Campaign — Orchestrator Journal

Started 2026-07-30 ~22:00 ET. Mandate: (P1) modernize the WASM demo to the new modular CPU prover, benchmark vs legacy WASM; (P2) WebGPU acceleration built from our own CUDA/Metal kernel designs — as fast as possible. Aim high.

## Mission map

- **Demo repo:** `~/dev/jolt-wasm-prover` (main @0da8984, deploys to jolt.rs). Legacy prover from a16z/jolt git default branch; local arkworks fork `~/dev/arkworks-algebra` branch `wasm` (WASM GLV/MSM opts); legacy bench: sha2 demo ~1.55s Chromium (12 threads), ~1.63s Safari.
- **New prover tree:** `~/dev/jolt` branch `perf/manycore-scaling` @70a294ad5 (contains perf/kernels-optimized @bf802c135 — verified ancestor). WORKSPACE RESTRUCTURED: no jolt-core; modular crates `jolt-prover`, `jolt-prover-legacy`, `jolt-verifier`, `jolt-witness`, `jolt-kernels` (+`-derive`, has `backend.rs`, `optimized/`, `reference/` modules), `jolt-dory`, `jolt-field`, `jolt-poly`, `jolt-sumcheck`, `jolt-transcript`, `jolt-r1cs`, `jolt-openings`, `jolt-claims`, `jolt-crypto`, `jolt-program`, `jolt-riscv`, `jolt-platform`, `jolt-sdk`, `jolt-inlines/*`, `tracer`, `common`. Arkworks pinned: a16z/arkworks-algebra branch `dev/twist-shout` (git). Dory: `dory-pcs` 0.4.0 crates.io. Examples incl. `examples/sha2-chain` = host-API rosetta stone.
- **Donors for P2:**
  - Metal: branch `gpu/metal-backend` @ef53a2c67 (worktree exists: `~/dev/jolt/.worktrees/gpu-metal-backend`, READ-ONLY — other campaign's). M4-mini close-out 16.75s @2^23 = 2.25× same-tree CPU; kernels in `crates/jolt-kernels/src/metal/` (slots, one-hot, batched inverse, device-resident Dory, coop Miller). Vendored dory-pcs on branch. `JOLT_METAL_DISABLE=1` = CPU-optimized arm same binary. Docs: `~/dev/jolt/.metal-m5-campaign-journal.md`, `.metal-m5-cuda-port-audit.md` (X-ticket port ests), `.metal-m5-w3-specs.md`.
  - CUDA: parity pin 98a6f3eba (commit exists locally, on NO branch — reach via `git show`/worktree add detached). 1.97s @2^23 / 5.00s @2^25 on 5090.
  - June WebGPU (old prover): demo repo branch `webgpu-e2e-bench` — full-GPU Dory (`gpu-pcs` crate), GPU-worker+SAB engine, coop Miller in WGSL, GLV/signed-MSM parity. RESULT: native wgpu 30.2s vs CPU 9.10s keccak 2^20; browser 877s vs 32.6s @2^20 — **browser ceiling = Tint's codegen of unrolled 16-bit-limb Montgomery WGSL (3-17× vs naga+Metal)**. Conclusion doc: vault `archive/jolt-gpu-conclusion-2026-06.md`. NOT final — kernel designs have changed completely — but do not repeat its dead ends.
  - Apple/naga trap: Metal miscompiles/hangs LARGE WGSL kernels (naga loop-bounding); fix = small kernels (~≤5 G1 point-ops inlined), multi-dispatch splits. (memory: naga-metal-loop-bounding-hang)
  - June deadlock pattern: engine-side rayon injecting into parked global pool → native private pool / wasm serial fallbacks. Tint first-compile ~10min/device (persist Chrome profile to cache Dawn shaders).

## Constraints & standing rules

- Executor: Jolt planning/implementing/reviewing = fable-max workers; research = fable-high, web-enabled. 200k ctx default.
- **Research lane scope (user correction Jul 30 21:52): do NOT hunt other teams' Jolt-on-WebGPU write-ups — internal, unpublished, unfindable. General WebGPU/WGSL engineering lit only** (limb strategies incl. public ZPrize WebGPU-MSM work, subgroup ops, buffer/binding limits, Tint/Dawn perf gotchas). Build the backend from our own CUDA/Metal designs.
- Gates: proof byte-parity vs CPU arm where CPU is byte-stable; prove+verify pass; honest benches (browser + deno/node-webgpu where applicable; report device + compiler stack). Canonical: largest scale fitting browser memory; report 2^20–2^23 minimum. WASM heap cap = 4GB (config.toml); WebGPU buffers live OUTSIDE the wasm heap → device residency also relieves memory ceiling.
- Hardware: this M4 Mac mini (10-core GPU, 16GB, 96GB/s). Chrome via chrome:true. Other campaigns run on OTHER boxes (5090, M5 Max) but share `~/dev/jolt` checkout READ-ONLY — never switch branches in the main tree or touch existing worktrees.
- Disk: 81GiB free at start; each worktree target/ = 5-16GB. `/bin/rm -rf` for real deletion (rm is aliased to rip = graveyard). Remove worktrees IMMEDIATELY when merged.
- Timed browser benches: mkdir-lock `/tmp/jolt-wasm-bench.lock.d`, quiet machine, ≥3 runs, medians.
- Dashboard: `~/.pika/web/reports/jolt-gpu-speed-data.js` (schema in file) — add `webgpu` campaign entry at first real numbers; every edit followed by `uv run python ~/.pika/web/reports/jolt-gpu-speed-sync.py`.
- No pushes. Commits in worktrees. Report to user only at: P1 complete w/ numbers, major P2 milestones, genuine blockers.

## User decisions

- **Jul 30 21:59 ET — full mandate confirmed.** (1) Internal (unpublished) Jolt WebGPU work WAS faster than his CPU-WASM prover → a well-built WebGPU backend beating CPU-WASM is achievable. The pessimistic Metal-campaign-era assessment (CPU-WASM ~25× faster than browser WebGPU ≈ June's 32.6s vs 877s @2^20) is a HYPOTHESIS TO FALSIFY, not a verdict. (2) Memory: bench whatever fits the 4 GiB buffer cap; expected ~2^22 max = **canonical scale**. Jolt small-space/streaming memory-reduction techniques can extend scale later — note, don't block. Mandate: build a proper new WebGPU backend, make it beat CPU-WASM.

- **Jul 30 22:50 ET — stage priorities + 13-bit follow-up.** (1) User's internal knowledge of the fast WebGPU prover: gains were in **COMMITMENT + OPENING-PROOF (PCS/Dory) stages, NOT sumchecks — but sumchecks were untried, not disproven**. Priority: commitments/openings = proven-win surface first, AND attack sumchecks. All of the above. (Aligns with W1d economics: Dory = EC ALU-dense = our 2-3× surface.) (2) 13-bit lazy-carry must not die as a negative: probe whether it wins elsewhere — Akita fp128 lattice field (p=2^128−275 Solinas, see ~/dev/fp128-gpu-bench RESULTS.md) or dies/backends where carries aren't free (ZPrize's M1/M3 data says they exist). Small probe lane (W1e), don't derail the main campaign.

### W1 results
- **W1b research r1 DONE (Jul 30 22:11 ET)** → `.webgpu-research-r1.md`. Top-5: (1) **13-bit lazy-carry Montgomery limbs** — ZPrize'23 winner: 4.2× vs 16-bit CIOS on M3 (88 vs 366ms), 5× on M1; root cause Apple RSHIFT32 ≈8cyc vs IMUL32 4cyc — carries are the bottleneck, not muls. (2) applegpu-disassemble one Tint kernel to check IMULHI32 re-fusion of the 16×16 hi idiom (~30min, only thing that could narrow 13-vs-16). (3) **node-webgpu (npm `webgpu`, Dawn) as CI harness** — exercises Tint→MSL = Chrome path; deno = naga (Metal-hang trap + 2.2× off Chrome dispatch perf). (4) Mega-dispatch drivers, ZERO per-round readbacks — mapAsync 5-15ms FLAT; 25 naive sumcheck rounds ≈ 125ms pure latency; one encoder/submit per phase; indirect dispatch for data-dependent bounds. (5) Bench flags `--enable-dawn-features=disable_robustness,disable_workgroup_init --enable-webgpu-developer-features` (robustness 17-30%, up to 3×; report flagged+unflagged).
- Architecture-changing: **limits non-issue** — M4 Chrome grants 4GiB−4 maxBufferSize/binding, probe allocated 18GiB live device buffers in-browser (device residency unlocks scale well past the wasm heap); shard >4GiB per binding. **CPU-driven per-round loops RULED OUT in browser** (ms-scale IPC floor) — validates micro-op-stream mega-dispatch; no cross-workgroup sync within a dispatch on Apple (relaxed atomics only, no forward progress). **Subgroups shipped** (Chrome 134, Apple width 32): 5.3× measured on register-resident reductions on this M4, ~0 DRAM-bound. Tint copies module-scope const arrays into per-thread memory → micro-op/window tables must be storage/uniform buffers. Dead ends: dot4U8Packed (no DP4a on Metal), f16/f32 FMA limbs (relaxed math → unsound), Barrett, WGSL u64/mulhi (parked since 2021), 16-bit muls (= 32-bit cost on Apple), grid-wide-sync uber-kernels.

### W1d (spawned Jul 30 22:12) — mont-mul WGSL microbench = the falsification instrument
13-bit lazy-carry vs 16-bit CIOS BN254 Fq mont-mul, node-webgpu (Tint) + Chrome, M4. Anchors: Metal native CIOS 1.65 Gmul/s device / CPU all-core 0.59 native. Go/no-go: Tint-path rate ≥ ~1 Gmul/s ⇒ GPU offload of ALU-bound stages beats CPU-WASM with margin.

### W1d outcome (Jul 30 22:45) — ORCHESTRATOR DECISIONS
- **13-bit hypothesis FALSIFIED on M4** (0.66× vs 16-bit; ZPrize 4-5× inverts — M4 carries free, mul count is the cost). **Campaign limb scheme = 16-bit CIOS** (width stays a codegen param). All arms bitwise-identical; node-webgpu CI harness validated; robustness flags ≈ 0% on ALU kernels → bench default Chrome.
- **MARGINAL-GO accepted, campaign proceeds:** 0.928 Gmul/s Tint roof = 1.57× CPU-native-all-core, ~2-3× CPU-WASM on ALU-dense stages; 0.56× Metal-native (u64/mulhi unreachable from WGSL — Tint tax 1.78× is structural). Win shape: ALU-dense stages (point ops, batched inversion, pairing, MSM/one-hot commit); raw-limb streaming folds are bandwidth-bound (0.40) BUT packed-8×u32 layout holds 0.93 even streaming → **packed limb layouts are a design requirement, not an optimization**.
- **June's kernel exonerated — its mont-mul ran AT the Tint roof.** The June 3-17× browser e2e gap was orchestration/readbacks or big-fused-kernel occupancy, NOT the primitive. This is P2 open question #1: architecture must be zero-readback, small-kernel, packed-layout by construction; W2 may add a cheap forensic pass on June's e2e trace if unexplained gaps persist.
- Dispatch economics on M4 Chrome: in-pass dispatch ~1µs, submit→done 0.17-0.20ms, mapAsync 5-15ms — submits are cheap, READBACKS are the enemy. Pipeline compile ≤400ms (no Tint blowup at kernel scale).
- Bench harness: `p2/wgsl-microbench` @ f999b17, `wgsl-bench/` (regression tool for all future kernels).

### W1d result (Jul 30 22:45) + orchestrator read
Full data in §Worker log W1d; harness = campaign regression tool (`p2/wgsl-microbench` @f999b17, `wgsl-bench/ node run-arm.mjs chrome-default`). **13-bit FALSIFIED on M4 (0.66× vs 16-bit — carries free on this die, cost = pure mul count; all widths hit the same ~490 G mul32/s roof). Limb scheme locked: 16-bit CIOS, width kept as codegen param.** Peak 0.928 Gmul/s device via Tint = 1.57× CPU-native-all-core / ~2× CPU-WASM / 0.56× Metal-native (Tint tax 1.78×, mulhi unreachable from WGSL). **MARGINAL-GO with sharp structure: ALU-dense stages (EC point ops, batched inversion, pairing, MSM) beat CPU-WASM ~2-3×; DRAM-streaming folds do NOT (0.40 Gmul/s at 97 GB/s — same unified bus the CPU uses).** June's exact kernel runs AT the roof under Tint ⇒ **June's 3-17× browser gap was composition/orchestration (big fused kernels: registers/occupancy; readbacks), never the primitive** — and our CUDA/Metal-derived designs (few phases, device-resident, micro-op streams) attack exactly that. Orchestration budget numbers: ~1 µs/dispatch in-pass, 0.17-0.20 ms submit→done, mapAsync readbacks = the enemy, pipeline compile ≤400 ms (no Tint blowup at kernel scale). Robustness flags ≈ 0% on ALU kernels → ship-default Chrome loses nothing. node-webgpu ≡ Chrome bitwise → CI harness validated.
**Strategic consequence:** whole-prove shape will be a hybrid split like Metal's (GPU takes ALU-dense pools, CPU-WASM keeps bandwidth-bound folds unless the 12-thread WASM arm can't saturate the bus — W2 must measure CPU-WASM streaming rate to place that boundary). Expected win class: Metal got 2.25× native-vs-native at 1.65 Gmul/s; we have 0.93 vs a WASM-taxed CPU arm — target ≥1.5-2× whole-prove over CPU-WASM at 2^20-2^22, refine after W1a's profile.

### W2a result (Jul 31 04:57) — **GO. Composition ceiling falsified; June's killer identified.**
Composition tax ≈5-7%, NO occupancy knee: binv 0.916 / xyzz 0.896 / madd 0.878 / **bucket-accumulate 0.865** / June's 9-Fq fold shape 0.838 Gmul/s-equiv (vs W1d roof 0.928); flat across wg 32-256 and 3→9 Fq live state. **GO at 1.44× the 0.6 threshold = 2.9× est CPU-WASM EC-effective; ~79M Jacobian mixed-adds/s in-browser (89.7M XYZZ, 124.6M dbl).** Commit-slot prescription: Jacobian madd-2007-bl w/ guards, rolled loop, ONE add-class op/iter, wg64, Fe8-packed streams, ≥64K threads; XYZZ = A/B alt (+12% ops, +64B/pt). **June's killer = a WALL not a knee: ≥3 inlined add-class bodies per loop body ⇒ 10⁴-10⁵× collapse (driver scratch/preemption-thrash; not compile-time/guards/size). June's shipped shapes were fine ⇒ their 27× was orchestration/readbacks + cold compile. HARD RULE all waves: ≤2 add-class inlines per loop body; rolled loops preferred.**

### W2c result (Jul 31 05:14) — boundary lane: P2 economics settled
- **Canonical re-anchor (zombie-gated, clean): 2^20 = 33.19s, 2^22 = 88.44s (0.047 MHz)** — W1a's window WAS zombie-degraded (−7.3/−10.7%). Clean A/B: legacy 2.845 / new 1.895 = **1.50×**. Dashboard P1 point corrected + synced by W2c. All future timed passes: ioreg GPU-util gate mandatory.

### W3-T4 — tier-1 + witness (mid-flight, Jul 31 13:30 ET) — X21 was already in the tree; the webgpu slot just didn't ride it

**Design finding that re-frames the ticket:** the trunk at T1's tip ALREADY contains the full X21 machinery in jolt-witness (`RandomAccessRows`, `collect_bundles_par`, `collect_range_into` — the index-parallel, double-buffer collect), and the OPTIMIZED CPU arm's commit path already uses it (`optimized/commitment.rs::commit_pipelined`: rayon::join overlaps extraction of superchunk k+1 with the commit grid of k). **The webgpu commit slot regressed to the barrier-style `stream_witnesses` chunk walk** (extract → consume strictly alternating; W2b landmine #6). So in-browser, the CPU arm pipelines witness extraction while the GPU arm serializes it against device bucket jobs + increment feeds — a big slice of T1's "st0 is 56–65% stream_witnesses" is that lost overlap, not missing X21 infrastructure. The port is therefore small and surgical, not a 130-line consumer rewrite.

**Built (jolt `webgpu/t4-tier1`, off bc8a92948):**
- @ffa1cf3fd attribution spans: `witness_extract` (chunk-walk deliver), `witness_collect` (collect_range_into — fires on BOTH arms' pipelined paths), `commit_consume` / `commit_hot_index` / `commit_hot_device` / `commit_inc_feed` in the webgpu consumer. Traced-vs-traced pairs only; anchors untraced (T1's asymmetric tracing-tax caveat respected).
- @09d211611 **Piece A — pipelined device commit** (`pump_pipelined` in webgpu/commitment.rs, mirror of optimized `commit_pipelined`): fed windows + order identical ⇒ commitments/hints byte-identical by construction. `commitPipeline: false` JS / `JOLT_WEBGPU_COMMIT_PIPELINE=0` native forces the old walk (A/B + preserved baseline for attribution). Memory note: front/back staging = 2 × 2^21 × 80 B ≈ +168 MB transient in st0 (CPU arm already pays exactly this).
- @eef391d2e **Piece C — inc-slot staging trim** (T3's ~512 MB flag): the four inc tables were concatenated into one host Vec (4·len·32 B = 512 MB @2^22) before `create_buffer_init` (which maps+copies again). Now: empty device buffer + 4 per-table offset `write_buffer`s — the wasm-heap concat AND wgpu's mapped shadow both gone; device layout unchanged, bytes unchanged.
- @001c4629d **Piece B — XYZZ bucket A/B pair** (BucketMaddXyzz madd-2008-s 8M+2S / BucketReduceXyzz add-2008-s + reduce-side conversion back to Jacobian ⇒ readback layout, host decode, bytes unchanged). Default OFF; `bucketXyzz: true` selects. In-situ decision at bench time per spec.
- Demo `w3/t4-verify` @8b4c4de: commitPipeline/bucketXyzz knobs, t4-run.sh (lock-IN-script + ioreg gate + served-hash assert + PID kills), t4-attrib.sh (watchdog 360 s for the known engine wedge).

**Gates so far (all green):** naga suite 171/171 (168 + 3 new; 4 skipped = T1's miller quarantine); Tint wgsl-check **16/16** incl. new `bucket_madd_then_reduce_xyzz` vector byte-exact vs an ark host mirror of the exact XYZZ op order; native byte gate **5 configs identical** (off / on-pipelined / on-barrier-walk / off-rerun / on-xyzz) on sha2 + sha2_chain, arm engaged (`webgpu arm active` ×2) in every on config; commit-slot e2e parity tests cover pipelined, barrier, and xyzz arms (nextest process-per-test isolates the global options).

**Ordering vs the measure-first mandate:** the bench lock has been held by siblings since 12:16 (T2a → T2b → W4-R queue ahead of me), so pieces A/B/C were built first (all are ticket-mandated regardless of attribution; the commitPipeline knob preserves the T1-tip barrier arm bit-for-bit, so the baseline attribution is still measurable on the same binary). Attribution block is QUEUED behind the lock (t4-run.sh polling): browser gate @2^16 → traced @2^22 triple (barrier / pipelined / CPU) → untraced anchors ×4 (barrier / pipelined / xyzz / CPU). Table lands here + message_parent the moment it's out.

| witness extraction | **~0.3–0.5s barrier (witness_extract <2% of stage); 1.6s pipelined collect, overlapped** | the "X21 pool" — extraction was ALREADY index-parallel |
| hot-index build | 0.46 worker-s | negligible |
| i128 inc feeds | 8.8 worker-s ≈ ~0.9s wall, overlapped | stays CPU (spec) |
| prime_prepared_cache | 21.9 worker-s ≈ ~2.2s | run-1 one-time (T1 stampede fix working — 1 build) |

**Verdicts that re-price the ledger:**
1. **T1's "st0 is 56–65% stream_witnesses = X21 pool (15.3s)" was consume-inside-the-walk, not witness collection.** Extraction is ~0.5s. The X21 witness-collect port as a *pool* is DEAD (nothing to win); Piece A (pipelining) is correct + free but nets only ~−0.2s st0 traced (barrier 27.34 → pipe 27.14; the −2.8s whole-prove traced delta is mostly window drift in st6b). Keep default ON (strictly-overlapping, byte-identical), but it is not the win.
2. **The real T4 pool: 80 × ~190 ms serialized device jobs = 15.1s st0 wall with a ~2.4s compute floor** — overhead-dominated (per-job SAB round-trip + 8 MB upload + 2 params writes + submit + mapAsync-in-busy-queue + decode). NEW ATTACK (in-scope, my file): **fuse each superchunk's 40 per-column jobs into ONE mega-job** — one job = 40 offset uploads into one device indices buffer + 80 dispatches (40 madd+reduce pairs, same per-dispatch geometry ⇒ no 65535-wg exposure) in ONE submit + ONE 15 MB readback. Est st0 15.1 → ~3s lane time (−12s class). XYZZ rides on top (A/B'd at final bench).
3. **T1's −8s tier-1 single-run signal: KILLED.** CPU-arm st0 28.84 vs GPU-arm 27.14–27.34 traced ⇒ the whole commit slot nets only ≈ −1.5s @2^22 on the T1 tree.
4. **T2b's net-negative @2^20 finding: mechanism CONFIRMED by my numbers** — the slot is per-job-overhead-bound; at 2^20 (40 jobs × ~150 ms overhead vs a ~2.5s CPU one-hot pool) it goes negative. Gate policy: re-measure @2^20 after fusion; if still negative, per-slot `min_terms_per_slot["commit"]` floor ≈ 2^21. (Their two landmines acknowledged: my dispatches are ≤2048 workgroups by construction — TARGET_THREADS-capped, unchanged by fusion; engagement is asserted by the browser gate + millerServed/arm-active on every on-run, and the 2^22 runs verify valid proofs = the ≥2^22 leg.)
5. **Piece C (inc staging trim) memory datum: arm-on wasm-heap delta vs CPU arm ≈ +4 MB** (2,737,897,472 vs 2,733,637,632 B, identical across all four GPU runs) — T3 measured **+1.08 GB** on their tree pre-trim. The 512 MB concat + its create_buffer_init shadow are gone; headroom @2^22 is no longer the binding worry (miller table remains device-side).

#### W3-T4 fusion BUILT (Jul 31 ~14:30 ET) — jolt @b504b5cf3, demo @00b33e9; all gates green; timed windows queued

Landed better than the sketch above: instead of 40 dispatch pairs, the madd kernels gained a **committed-column dimension** (`params.flags` = windows per column, column decoded from the thread id — a single-column call is the identical geometry, so probes and callers degrade cleanly), and the consumer uploads ONE flat column-major index block per superchunk. **80 jobs → 2 jobs; each job = 1 upload + 1 madd dispatch + 1 reduce dispatch + one 15.7 MB readback.** Fused shape @2^22 = 164 K threads ⇒ ~2.6 K workgroups (loud assert on the 1-D 65535 limit — T2b's landmine can't bite silently). CPU increment feeds overlap the device job via rayon::join. Per-window scan order unchanged ⇒ outputs byte-identical.

Gates on the fused build: naga 172/172 (4 = miller quarantine skips); Tint wgsl-check **16/16** with BOTH bucket vectors upgraded to the fused 2-column decode against exact ark host mirrors; new `bucket_kernels_sum_known_points_fused_cols` naga KAT (cols=3, both kernel pairs); native byte gate — off / fused-pipelined / fused-barrier-walk / fused-xyzz all IDENTICAL on sha2 + sha2_chain, arm engaged ×2 per config. Demo gained `minTermsCommit` knob (per-slot gate for the commit-slot-off A/B arm the T2b sign-reproduction needs). Final timed windows queued behind the W4-R soak: A = @2^22 (gate, off ×3 / on ×3 medians, xyzz + commit-gated singles, traced pair), B = @2^20 (off/on ×3 + slot-sign arms).

#### W3-T4 CSR pivot (Jul 31 ~15:20 ET) — the fused SCAN was a regression; the divergence diagnosis + X27 sorted-runs fix

First timed window on the fused-scan build convicted it: **commit_hot_device n=2 at 15.5 s/job (31 s of lane vs 15.1 pre-fusion; st0 44.9 traced)**. Root cause is not overhead at all — **the guarded 1-in-K scan is branch-divergence-bound** (~1/16 lane efficiency on the add), so the old 80 per-column jobs at ~190 ms were already compute-bound and my "~30 ms floor" used a full-lane rate that the shape can't reach. The fusion also collapsed `segments` 16 → 1 (8192-position per-thread scans), doubling the damage. **Fix = CUDA X27's sorted-segment recipe (@9b317bcbe):** the consumer counting-sorts each `(committed column, window)` block's positions by hot address on the CPU (stable ascending — bucket addition order matches the scan kernels'; ~10K independent 8192-element sorts, parallel, overlapped behind the device job), `BucketMaddCsr(+Xyzz)` walks each bucket's CONTIGUOUS run split 16 ways — every lane adds, zero guard misses. Segment partial boundaries differ from the scan kernels' (representation-only; the tier-2 absorb batch-normalizes — pinned by slot-vs-optimized tests + native byte gates). Scan kernels stay as probe/A-B subjects. Gates re-run on CSR: naga 172/172, **Tint 18/18** (both CSR vectors byte-exact vs op-order-exact ark mirrors, hoisted to a shared `mirror` module), native bytes off/csr-on/csr-walk/csr-xyzz IDENTICAL ×2 proofs, arm engaged everywhere.

### W3-T2a — trunk drift re-derivation notes for T2b (Jul 31 07:50 ET, mid-flight)

How the Metal seam transplanted onto the drifted trunk (lazy_ra family; T2b faces the same class on instruction_read_raf's 932-line drift):

1. **The hooks transplant, the depth doesn't.** Metal's tree binds lazily to width 4 and materializes at the THIRD bind (T/8); trunk binds to width 8 and materializes at the FOURTH (T/16, deliberate change — trunk header: "T/16 beats T/8 on both axes on a 64-thread host"). Keep TRUNK depth: the CPU fallback state machine defines what "recompute the same round from unchanged state" means, so the seam must not move the switch. Cost: one extra gather width (w8 kernel case) + materialize gathers 16 offsets — both just parameters in the WGSL; the driver trait docs change from cycles/8 to cycles/16.
2. **Metal's launch/collect (begin_round/collect_round overlap tier) does NOT exist on trunk's ProveRounds** — dropped it entirely; sync `device_lanes` only. Step0's 0.7–1.4 ms round floor makes the overlap ~worthless in-browser anyway.
3. **Trunk jolt-poly already has everything the seam needs**: `e_in_current`/`e_out_current`/`gruen_poly_from_evals`/`gruen_poly_deg_3` all public — zero jolt-poly changes. Row mapping verified: `row = (x_out << in_bits) | x_in`, so device eq indexing is `e_out[gid >> log_in] · e_in[gid & mask]` exactly; the booleanity `new_with_scaling` scalar rides the host ℓ recompose, never the device.
4. **Consumer seam shape that survives drift**: factory closures on the PREPARE fronts (`prepare_booleanity_cycle(session, witness, inputs, |device_inputs| Option<driver>)`), `new_with_driver` where the kernel ctor is directly constructible, `ensure_host()` before ANY direct table access (output_claims, final_values). The optimized kernel stays THE kernel; a `None` factory is the CPU build. Grep-anchors for T2b: `device_lanes(`, `ensure_host(`, `LazyRaDevice`.
5. **Parity mechanics that carried over unchanged**: device sums plain Fr in any order (deferred-reduction accumulators are exact mod p, distributivity exact) ⇒ byte-identical lanes; per-round branch-table re-upload is fine (production chunk_bits = 4 → 16-entry tables, hundreds of KB per round worst case); canonical-vs-Montgomery is a non-issue here (all Fr Montgomery end-to-end — the CUDA canonical-limb landmine is T3's, not ours).
6. **Two Tint-only traps hit**: `meta` and `active` are RESERVED WGSL keywords — naga accepts them, Tint rejects (renamed pmeta/live). Run the wgsl-check tier before ANY browser attempt.
7. **Real dims on the demo guests** (from the forced native run): instruction RAV num_committed=32, batch=4, chunk_bits=4 (small OneHotConfig); booleanity 40 polys (32 instr + 4 bytecode + 4 ram); ram RAV num_committed=4. Uploads tiny; RAV kernel register ceiling (JK_RAV_MAX_BATCH=8, 26 Fe static arrays) untested above batch=4 — probe before lifting MAX_BATCH.

(Ambient walls this window ran hot — loadavg 4.6→13, 3 login sessions; the paired delta is the deliverable; wave-close re-anchor owns absolutes.)

Remaining st6b: BytecodeReadRafCycle 1.53 s (the deliberate follow-up cut) + prepares/host tails.

**The bug that mattered (fixed @069991afe): `RaMaterialize` breached WebGPU's 65,535 workgroups-per-dimension cap at 2^22** (poly×len flat grid = 65,536 with 32 committed polys — ONE over). On wasm the breach is an ASYNC validation error: the dispatch silently drops, the dense tables stay garbage, and the Gruen consumers' q(0)-from-previous-claim recovery self-heals every round so nothing trips until the stage-6b final output-fold check. Native ≤2^19 (8,192 wgs) and browser ≤2^21 never breach — a scale-shaped silent-corruption class the whole campaign must respect: **every dispatch shape needs a per-dimension-cap audit at 2^22+ scales; naga/native tests cannot catch it.** Fix: 2D grid (x = dense index, workgroup row per poly), debug_assert on the 1D helper, fail-closed decline above the cap in round admission/adoption.

**Bench-integrity incidents (protocol hardened):** (a) **server hijack** — T1's `server.mjs` bound my port 8093 at 08:15 after a cross-lane `pkill -f server.mjs`; every browser number 09:26–10:20 measured THEIR build (their `miller_webgpu` spans in my traces). All such numbers voided. New rule shipped in `t2a-final-set.sh` and adopted by T2b: **unique port per lane + served-wasm hash must equal pkg hash before ANY run + kill servers by PID only.** (b) Same-session repeat-prove hang + the +4.2 s "regression" @2^22 that triggered the whole investigation were artifacts of the hijacked build/memory pressure, but the hunt surfaced the two REAL fixes above (workgroup cap, upload transients). (c) macOS has no `timeout`(1) — scripts using it silently no-op with exit 127.

**Follow-ups for the merge owner / next wave:** (1) BytecodeReadRafCycle twin (1.53 s @2^22 remaining) — design in §drift notes: inc-style twin owning a device `combined` ping-pong from round 0 + the ra gather family, host fallback via a `from_bound_state` ctor; (2) packed-rows buffer currently persists on-device between proves (generation-tagged, replaced per trace) — fine at 200 MB, ledger it when T2b shares the handle; (3) HANDOFF_LEN default 2^12 is right per step0 economics (band 2^12–2^15); per-slot sweep left undone (sub-1% class at current shapes); (4) the wave-close re-anchor should measure this branch's off-walls fresh — trunk-lineage off @2^22 ranged 59.4–87.3 s across windows today (thermal/login-session spread), paired deltas were stable throughout.

### W3-T2a step0 — inc slot round latency (Jul 31 06:30 ET) — **browser round floor is ~0.7–1.4 ms, NOT 5–15 ms; break-even ≈ 2^14, 128× below the 2.4M prior**

Method: live inc_claim_reduction slot on the consolidated trunk (jolt 585362bfb + tracing spans around `device_round`/`materialize_host_tail`; demo b4a7fe3, worktree w3-t2a-verify), sha2-chain @2^16 (17 iters) and @2^18 (69 iters), headless system Chrome, 10T, minTerms=1, tracing on; spans extracted per round from the Perfetto JSON (`step0-inc.mjs`, committed on w3/t2a-verify). Bench lock held, ioreg GPU-util 0% at start; loadavg 2.8→7.5 across the sweep (T1/T3 compiling) — device-round medians stable across runs, CPU baseline co-loaded (marked).

**Per-round DEVICE wall (submit + 2-dispatch fused bind+eval + 64 B readback through SAB/gpu-worker), steady-state by pre-bind table length:**

| len | 128 | 1K | 4K | 8K | 32K | 65K | 131K | 262K |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ms/round | 0.74 | 0.82 | 0.96 | 1.08 | 1.45 | 1.53–1.66 | 2.12 | 3.08 |

- **Model: round ≈ 0.74 ms floor + ~3.4 ns/element** (traffic-shaped: 4 tables × len × 32 B ≈ bandwidth at 90 GB/s). The 5–15 ms mapAsync floor DOES NOT APPLY on this path — the readback's mapAsync runs inside the dedicated GPU worker whose event loop is empty; the W1b/W1d floor was a busy-main-thread artifact. **Every round-serial threshold in the W3 specs was priced 7–14× too pessimistic.**
- First-ever device round of the slot: +5–15 ms one-time (buffer/pipeline first-touch; 7.97 ms @2^16, 14.8 ms @2^18) — amortize or pre-touch at warmup if a slot's total is small.
- Handoff readback (`from_bound_tables` materialization): 0.9–1.6 ms at len 64–4096 (8 KB–512 KB) — cheap, as predicted.
- CPU inc rounds baseline (minTerms=2^30, SAME box but loadavg 7.5 — inflated): 19 rounds sum 57.9 ms @2^18, biggest round ~20 ms (clean-estimate ~11 ms at 0.149 Gmul/s). Device arm total for the same slot: ~16 ms steady (13 rounds) + 0.9 ms handoff.
- **Break-even for the inc summand (c ≈ 6.4 mul-eq/element): device round(L) = CPU round(L) at L ≈ 2^14** (0.74 ms ÷ (43 − 3.4) ns/element ≈ 19K). In c·L terms: **≈ 100–200 K mul-eq/round, not 2.4 M**. DEFAULT_HANDOFF_LEN 2^12 is nearly right in-browser; the useful sweep band is **2^12–2^15** (not 2^14–2^19). Heavier summands (rav c ≈ 20–50) break even at L ≈ 2^11–2^12 — the fold lane can ride the device essentially to the structural floor.
- T2b/T3 calibration: price a round-serial device ladder at **~0.8–1.5 ms/round overhead + streamed-bytes/90 GB/s**; one 64 B eval readback per round is FINE (22 rounds ≈ 20–30 ms total tax at 2^22, not 220–330 ms). Phase-boundary one-shot readbacks (st5 scans) are ~1 ms class, not 10 ms.

### W1d — mont-mul microbench (DONE Jul 30 ~23:0x ET) — 13-bit HYPOTHESIS FALSIFIED on M4; June's kernel exonerated

Harness: `wgsl-bench/` on branch `p2/wgsl-microbench` (worktree `.worktrees/p2-wgsl-microbench`). BN254 Fq, KATs vs BigInt reference (edge vectors + max-limb lazy-overflow stress + 16-mul chains) green for all 6 kernels × 4 arms before timing; GPU-timestamp medians, ≥3 reps, dependent-chain shape (65,536 threads × ~400-mul chains, unroll ×4), no lock contention.

**Chain throughput, Gmul/s device (best wg = 64 everywhere), kernel × arm:**

| kernel | mul32/mul | node-default | node-flagged | chrome-default | chrome-flagged |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| cios16_june (June verbatim) | 528 | 0.915 | 0.914 | 0.913 | 0.913 |
| cios16_lit (literal modulus) | 528 | 0.920 | 0.920 | 0.922 | 0.919 |
| lazy15u (NSAFE=2) | 595 | 0.816 | 0.815 | 0.816 | 0.816 |
| lazy14u (NSAFE=8) | 741 | 0.680 | 0.681 | 0.679 | 0.683 |
| lazy13u (ZPrize opt, unrolled) | 820 | 0.603 | 0.601 | 0.601 | 0.603 |
| lazy13r (ZPrize opt, rolled) | 820 | 0.596 | 0.596 | 0.594 | 0.596 |

Peak with 262,144 threads: **cios16 = 0.928 Gmul/s** (all four arms identical); 65K threads = 97% of that; 4K threads = 0.61.

**The one-line explanation: every kernel lands on the same mul32 roofline — 482–503 G mul32/s ≈ the machine's ~494 G/s mul32 issue rate.** On M4 (unlike M1/M3 where RSHIFT32≈8cyc made carries dominate), carry chains are free; mont-mul cost = multiply count, so 16-bit CIOS (528 muls) beats 13-bit lazy (820 muls) by exactly the mul-count ratio.

Verdicts:
1. **13-bit vs 16-bit via Tint on M4: 0.66× — ZPrize's 4.2–5× does NOT reproduce; it INVERTS.** Limb-width choice for this campaign: 16-bit CIOS, unless a target GPU measures otherwise (keep width a codegen parameter; the sweep is now a 1-command regression: `node run-arm.mjs chrome-default`).
2. **GO/NO-GO: MARGINAL-GO. Best Tint rate 0.928 Gmul/s** vs threshold ~1.0: 7% short. vs CPU-native all-core 0.59 = **1.57×**; CPU-WASM sits below 0.59, so ALU-bound GPU stages beat CPU-WASM by ~1.6–2× — real margin, not the 2.8× the Metal-native anchor (1.65, mulhi-CIOS unreachable from WGSL; Tint tax = 1.78×) would suggest. GPU offload pays only for ALU-dense stages (point ops, batched inversion, pairings), not field-mul-throughput-bound folds.
3. **Robustness-flag delta ≈ 0%** (both harnesses) — the 17–30% literature number does not apply to constant-index unrolled ALU kernels; default-Chrome users lose nothing here.
4. **node-webgpu ≡ Chrome: rates ≤1% apart, outputs bitwise identical across all 4 arms** (KAT buffers + fixed-k checksums). node-webgpu validated as the campaign's CI harness.

Campaign-critical side findings:
- **June's browser ceiling hypothesis is DEAD: the exact June kernel (var<private> modulus, unrolled 16-bit CIOS) runs at the mul32 roof under Tint** — 0.91 Gmul/s, 55% of native-Metal-with-mulhi. The 3–17× browser-vs-naga gap in June's e2e was NOT the mont-mul primitive; suspects now: big-kernel composition (register pressure/occupancy in fused point-op kernels), dispatch orchestration, or naga-vs-Tint divergence on LARGE kernels only. P2 kernel work can reuse June's field core style safely.
- var<private> const arrays: free (lit vs june <1%). Rolled vs unrolled: free (<1.5%). Metal unrolls + register-promotes fine at mont-mul size — codegen style is unconstrained at this scale.
- Streaming (1 mul/element, 2^20): raw-20-word layout saturates DRAM at 97 GB/s → 0.40 Gmul/s; packed-8-word: june8 0.93 Gmul/s @ 89 GB/s (ALU/BW balanced), packed13 0.60 (ALU-capped). Fold-style kernels are bandwidth-bound → limb width irrelevant there; keep boundary buffers packed 8×u32.
- Dispatch overhead: ~1.0–1.3 µs per dispatch inside a pass; submit→onSubmittedWorkDone 0.17 ms native / 0.20 ms browser (min 0.10) — compute-only submits are NOT the 5–15 ms mapAsync horror; orchestration budget: batch dispatches per pass, readbacks remain the enemy.
- Pipeline compile worst case 400 ms (June unrolled module), everything else ≤136 ms; macOS shader cache makes same-source recompiles ~0 ms across processes. No Tint compile blowup at microbench scale.

Falsification instrument disposition: kernels + harness committed on `p2/wgsl-microbench` under `wgsl-bench/` (`run-arm.mjs <arm>`, `summarize.mjs`; results/*.json = evidence). ZPrize reference WGSL vendored under `wgsl-bench/zprize-ref/` for provenance.

### W1e — 13-bit probe (DONE Jul 30 ~23:20 ET) — fp128 measured in WGSL; 13-bit decision table

**Q1: Akita fp128 (p = 2^128−275, Solinas, plain mul NOT Montgomery) via Tint on M4.** Suite committed @520d34a on `p2/wgsl-microbench` (`wgsl-bench/ node run-arm.mjs <arm> --suite fp128`; new fp128-{ref,kernels,jobs}.mjs, executor untouched). KATs vs BigInt mod-p green before timing (canonical boundaries + all-limbs-max redundant-rep stress held on every chained mul + 16-mul chains, exact match after in-kernel canonicalization); chrome-default ≡ node-webgpu bitwise, rates ≤0.5% apart; GPU-timestamp medians of 3 × 80–123 ms passes under the bench lock, loadavg 1.9. Full section with method appended to `~/dev/fp128-gpu-bench/RESULTS.md`.

| kernel | mul32/mul | peak Gmul/s (Tint) | roofline placement |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| school16 (8×16 comba + ×275 fold) | 73 | **6.66** | 486 G mul32/s |
| lazy13 (10×13 lazy + ×1100 fold) | 112 | 4.38 | 490 G mul32/s |

- **13v16 for fp128 = 0.657× = the mul-count ratio (0.652). Same law as BN254, second field shape: on M4 carries never contend with mul issue; cost = pure mul32 count.** No register/ILP surprise from fp128's 4×-smaller state — both kernels pin the same ~490 G mul32/s roof as the BN254 sweep (zero codegen slack).
- WGSL-vs-native-MSL tax for fp128 = **1.70×** (6.66 vs 11.33 G native sol-4×32) — structural, same as BN254's 1.78×: mulhi32/u64 unreachable from WGSL (73 vs 42 mul-class ops).
- Intermediate widths skipped on math: any W<16 raises L² product muls while fold stays ~L (9×15 = 92, 11×12 = 121+; 10×14 fold const overflows u32) — 16-bit dominates wherever rate = roof/mul-count.
- **Headline for Akita/lattice: in-browser fp128 mul = 6.66 G/s on base M4 = 2.8–2.9× CPU all-core asm roof (2.28–2.42 G), 14× 1T, 8.6× generic-CIOS CPU.** Browser-GPU offload of lattice field arithmetic is plausible on this die class — but only for shapes ALU-bound below ~14 B/mul traffic (96 GB/s ÷ 6.66 G); streaming shapes stay bus-parity (one unified 96 GB/s pie, per W-F).

**Q2 decision table — limb-scheme codegen-param policy per die/backend:**

| die/backend | carry cost vs mul32 | limb scheme | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Apple M1/M3 (WGSL) | RSHIFT32 8cyc = 2× IMUL32 4cyc; carry chain ≈ 2.7 mul-eq | **13-bit lazy** | ZPrize'23 winner (Tal Derei + Koh Wei Jie), measured in-browser: 13-bit 88 ms vs 16-bit CIOS 366 ms on M3 (4.2×), 129 vs 645 ms on M1 (5×) — links in `.webgpu-research-r1.md` §1 (z-prize/2023-entries, HackMD, philipturner/metal-benchmarks for the 8-cyc RSHIFT) |
| Apple M4 (this box) | ~0 — measured: every width lands on one ~490 G mul32/s issue roof | **16-bit eager** (CIOS for Montgomery, half-word schoolbook for Solinas) | W1d BN254 13v16 = 0.66×; W1e fp128 = 0.657×; both exactly the mul-count ratio |
| Apple M5 (Apple10) | **unmeasured** — `~/dev/fp128-gpu-bench/m5-results-*.md` contain int8 tensor-unit probes only, no scalar shift/mul roofs | default 16-bit; run the 1-command sweep on-box before trusting | M5's actual lever is the NA int8 GEMM (18–29 T MAC8/s raw; fp128 limb-GEMM 2.97 G mul-eq/s bit-exact; SIS-shape verdict "collapses to shader class") — a matmul-shaping question, not a limb-width one; no WGSL path to it anyway |
| NVIDIA via Dawn (Vulkan/D3D) | IMAD full-rate on mul pipes; IADD3/LOP3 carries on parallel pipes ≈ free | **15/16-bit eager** (predicted, unverified locally) | r1 doc: "15-bit won on NVIDIA" (mitschabaude/montgomery lineage); first-principles matches M4 shape: mul count rules |
| **general rule** | — | **13-bit lazy iff carry-class ops contend with multiply issue at ≥~2× mul32 cost; else widest ≤16-bit limb — mul count rules** | lazy costs +55% muls (BN254 528→820, fp128 73→112); operational test on any new die = run the sweep: all widths collapsing onto one mul32 roof ⇒ 16-bit; 13-bit matching/beating 16-bit ⇒ carries contended, go lazy |

**Verdict:** 13-bit lazy-carry is not dead — it is M1/M3-shaped (any die where shift/carry chains contend with mul issue at ≥2× cost); on M4 it loses by exactly its extra mul count on both field shapes now measured (BN254 Montgomery 0.66×, fp128 Solinas 0.657×), and fp128's smaller state doesn't change that. **Main-campaign kernel plans UNCHANGED: 16-bit CIOS stays the BN254 scheme, limb width stays a codegen param with the table above as policy.** The new result is ammunition for the Akita/lattice lane (browser-GPU fp128 = 2.8× CPU-all-core on ALU-dense shapes), not a course change for P2.

### W1a — phase 1 (Jul 31 04:00 ET) — modular CPU prover in-browser: e2e VALID, legacy A/B won, 2^22 ceiling confirmed

**Status: COMPLETE.** Demo ported to the NEW modular prover (`jolt_prover::prove` over `JoltBackend::<Fr, DoryScheme>::optimized()`), proving+verifying in Chrome. Branches: demo `phase1/new-prover` @d87ec4d (worktree `.worktrees/phase1-new-prover`); jolt `webgpu/jolt-base` @f28761453 (1 patch on 70a294ad5); arkworks `webgpu/wasm-minimal` @b811fb6a (1 patch on dev/twist-shout@76bb3a45, worktree `~/dev/arkworks-algebra/.worktrees/webgpu-wasm-minimal`).

**Scale ceiling = 2^22** (3.19 GB peak of 4 GiB), exactly the campaign expectation. 2^23 needs the small-space/streaming levers (out of scope P1). Sub-linear scaling: kHz still climbing at 2^22 (44 vs 37.7 at 2^21) — fixed overheads (tracing+witness in the timed window) still amortizing; per-cycle cost hasn't flattened.

**How the port works (architecture):**
- `src/engine.rs` = the whole story: ship `{name}_prover.bin` (= legacy `prover_prep.generators`, i.e. dory-pcs `ArkworksProverSetup`, ark-serialize UNCOMPRESSED for fast load) + `{name}_verifier.bin` (= modular `jolt_verifier::JoltVerifierPreprocessing<DoryScheme, Pedersen<Bn254G1>>`, bincode2/serde) + ELF. Browser: wrap SRS in `jolt_dory::DoryProverSetup(pub inner)`, bincode-decode verifier prep, assemble `jolt_prover::JoltProverPreprocessing { verifier, pcs_setup, committed_program: None }` → `JoltProgram::from_elf_bytes` → `TracerBackend::new().trace(...)` (MemoryConfig mirrored off `verifier.program.memory_layout()`) → `ProverConfig::derive::<Fr>` → pad to `config.trace_length` → `TraceBackend::new(JoltVmWitnessConfig::new(log_t, ram_K, one_hot_config), JoltVmWitnessInputs::new(&program, program_prep, padded))` → `prove::<Fr, DoryScheme, Pedersen<Bn254G1>, LegacyBlake2bTranscript, _>`. Verify: `jolt_verifier::verify` on bincode proof + bincode JoltDevice. Rosetta: `crates/jolt-prover/examples/modular_benchmark.rs` (this IS the modular pipeline; jolt-sdk macros still run the LEGACY prover — `build_prover_*` is not the modular path).
- generate.rs (native): guest macro fns `compile_X` + `preprocess_shared_X` (legacy shared prep), then generic `jolt::JoltProverPreprocessing::new(shared)` (sizes SRS off max_trace_length via `compute_max_total_vars`) + `verifier_preprocessing_from_prover` (legacy→modular conversion, lives in jolt-prover-legacy::zkvm::proof). SRS pair consistent by construction. sha2-chain guest at `max_trace_length = 2^23` → SRS only 3.1 MB, verifier prep 342 KB (vs legacy demo's 21 MB pkg-era blobs — the new artifacts are TINY).
- Inline registration is now inventory-based (link-time ctors via `jolt_inlines_sdk::register_inlines!`); `__wasm_call_ctors` runs them at instantiation — `init_inlines()` kept as a no-op export so worker.js is unchanged.

**wasm32 blockers found (only two!) + fixes:**
1. `jolt-kernels/src/optimized/registers_read_write.rs` `CoeffLut::saturated()`: `len*len` at the 65536-entry table = 2^32 → wraps to 0 on 32-bit usize → Indexed→Direct switch never fires → next bind's `(0..n*n)` empty → zero-length LUTs → all-thread index-OOB panics. Fixed in u64 (jolt @f28761453). Native 64-bit unaffected — THIS is why native roundtrip passed while the browser exploded; expect more of this class at bigger scales (`1usize << (log_k_chunk + log_t)` sites are fine ≤2^23 but tight).
2. Vanilla dev/twist-shout ark-ec MSM (`msm_bigint_wnaf`) builds a nested `rayon::ThreadPoolBuilder` per chunk → `build().unwrap()` panics on wasm (one fixed global pool only). Minimal fix: wasm cfg runs `msm_bigint_wnaf_parallel` directly on the global pool (arkworks @b811fb6a). The full June GLV/MSM fork port remains a later wave; profile first.
- NOT blockers: tracer/std (object crate parses in-memory), jolt-dory (has wasm32 cfg in setup_prover; urs_lock native-only), rayon par_iter/par_chunk_by (fine on wasm-bindgen-rayon), getrandom (0.2 js feature suffices), allocative 0.3.6, inventory ctors (fire on wasm).

**Build facts (exact recipe):**
- Toolchain: plain `nightly` (1.99.0-nightly 2026-07-27; tree needs ≥1.95 — old pin nightly-2025-12-01 is too old). rust-toolchain.toml targets wasm32-unknown-unknown + riscv64imac.
- Dep wiring: path deps into the jolt worktree; `dory = { package = "dory-pcs", version = "0.4.0", features=["backends"] }` + `ark-serialize = "0.5.0"` as direct deps. **CRITICAL: the jolt workspace uses a `[replace]` section (Cargo.toml line ~189, greps for "patch" miss it!) mapping registry ark-*:0.5.0 → the a16z fork — that's the one-ark-world trick that makes dory-pcs (registry ark) types satisfy fork trait bounds.** The demo mirrors it with `[patch.crates-io]` + `[patch.'https://github.com/a16z/arkworks-algebra']` onto the local patched arkworks worktree. Also: seed demo Cargo.lock from the jolt tree's lock before first build (fresh resolution picks incompatible registry versions — light-poseidon/PrimeField mismatch errors in jolt-transcript are the symptom).
- `.cargo/config.toml`: dropped `-C lto=fat` rustflag (breaks any rlib target; profile `lto=true` covers the cdylib), added `--export=__heap_base` (wasm-bindgen 0.2.114's threads transform requires it). Lib is cdylib-only; test-roundtrip includes engine.rs via `#[path]`.
- `wasm-pack build --release --target web` with `CARGO_UNSTABLE_BUILD_STD="panic_abort,std"` — ~2.5-6 min.
- **GOTCHA that cost an hour: server.mjs has an in-memory `compressCache` keyed by path with no mtime validation — RESTART the server after every wasm rebuild or you test stale bytes.** (Panic line numbers not moving after a patch = this.)
- Playwright: chromium download unreachable from this box (CDN 100% loss); use `channel:'chrome'` (system Chrome). bench.mjs's old clear-then-observe hung (output element doesn't exist pre-first-log); now counts 'Proof generated' occurrences.
- Native: `cargo run --release --features native --bin generate-preprocessing` then `--bin test-roundtrip` (runs the full modular prove+verify from the shipped bytes natively — catches everything except 32-bit-isms).

**For W2 (perf notes):** the timed in-browser window includes tracer emulation + witness materialization (same as legacy demo's window). wasm_tracing.rs (Perfetto) still wired — `init_tracing`/`get_trace_json` exports work, trace-capture.mjs should work unchanged for stage-level profiles. jolt-witness serial `visit_chunks` choke (W1c §2) untouched — X21 port is a shared P1/P2 ticket that should lift ALL ladder rungs. Frontend React UI still only exposes sha2/keccak tabs; sha2-chain is driven via worker.js directly (bench-chain.mjs pattern — init → load-program → prove/verify message loop).

### W2a — composed-EC falsifier (DONE Jul 31 ~05:0x ET) — GO: composition tax ≈6%, bucket-add 0.865; the June killer found and it is NOT occupancy

Harness: `wgsl-bench/` ec suite on `p2/wgsl-microbench` (@b33e0a8 suite, @20dbbc5 results). `node run-arm.mjs <arm> --suite ec [--profile safe|risky]`; `ec-probe.mjs` = isolated per-config probe. KATs limb-exact vs BigInt reference (real BN254 G1 points, all madd/xyzz guard paths incl. Z=0/H=0-double/infinity, identity flows, RCB ladder on G and pseudo points; reference itself self-tested against group law across 3 coordinate systems + endomorphism) — green on node-webgpu AND Chrome before any timing. Metric: **Gmul/s-equiv counts fe_mont_mul calls (S=M; adds/mul9/loads free)** — directly comparable to W1d's 0.928 roof. GPU timestamps, medians of 3, mkdir-lock + (new) ioreg device-utilization gate. Loadavg 1.5-2.6 (W1a CPU co-load; GPU-quiet windows verified by probe before/after each timed batch).

**Composed kernels sit AT the roof. There is no occupancy knee anywhere in the shippable envelope.**

Chrome-default, n=65536, u1, Gmul/s-equiv (node-default ≤1% apart everywhere):

| kernel (mul-eq/op, live state) | wg32 | wg64 | wg128 | wg256 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| binv control (2/iter, 3 Fq) | 0.916 | 0.915 | 0.907 | 0.906 |
| xyzz madd chain (10, 6 Fq) | 0.895 | 0.896 | 0.889 | 0.887 |
| madd_nogd chain (11, 5 Fq) | 0.882 | 0.883 | 0.875 | 0.874 |
| madd chain guarded (11, 5 Fq) | 0.874 | 0.878 | 0.866 | 0.866 |
| dbl chain (7, 3 Fq) | 0.868 | 0.872 | 0.863 | 0.864 |
| **bucket_accumulate K=64 (11, streamed Fe8 points)** | 0.863 | **0.865** | 0.861 | 0.854 |
| june_fold (RCB 8/12, 9 Fq acc+p1+p2) | 0.835 | 0.838 | 0.836 | 0.824 |

Unroll: dbl u2/u4 = u1 (0.86-0.87), dbl u8 probe 0.86 — dbl-class unrolling free to 8×. madd u2 = u1 (0.87); xyzz u2 ≈ 0.9. Thread ladder (wg64): 4096→0.658, 16384→0.827, 65536→0.878, 262144→0.888 (binv 0.930 = W1d peak); same saturation shape as W1d. Point-op rates @65536: **79 M mixed-adds/s (Jacobian), 89.7 M/s (XYZZ), 124.6 M dbls/s**. Streaming bucket = chain rate −1% at 5.5 B/mul-eq traffic (Fe8-packed). Guards ≤1%. Robustness-default Chrome loses nothing (matches W1d).

**The cliff that DOES exist (the real June-class killer): ≥3 inlined add-class bodies per rolled loop body → 10^4-10^5× collapse.** madd u3 (33 CIOS bodies of straight-line code, NO guards) and u4: n=4096,k=4 = 721K muls took **83.7-116s ≈ 6-9 Kmul/s (~10^5× below roof)**; zombie-drain data gives u3 ≈ 26 Kmul/s. Same wall for XYZZ u3. Properties: NOT compile-time (PSO ≤450ms); NOT guards (nogd collapses); NOT total code size (dbl u8 = 56 inlined mul bodies runs at roof; madd u3 = 33 dies); NOT wg-size or thread-count occupancy (collapse at n=1024..65536, all wg); wildly nonlinear in k (n=1024: k=1 16ms, k=4 >90s). Signature says driver-level pathology (scratch/preemption thrash class), not graceful spill: the wall is binary — one step over and kernels are unusable, which is exactly the failure mode a single fused "do-more-per-thread" kernel design walks into blind. dbl-class is exempt to ≥8 inlines (lighter per-body live set).

**June re-attribution, sharpened:** June's own shipped shapes — field core (W1d), and now their exact glv_scale_add composition (RCB complete formulas, 9 Fq persistent, rolled ladder, full 12-mul adds, Fe8 loads) — all run at 0.82-0.84 under Tint at every wg. **June's browser collapse was NOT the primitive, NOT their curve-kernel composition, NOT occupancy.** The only kernel-level catastrophic mechanism this die exhibits is the ≥3-add-inline wall. Remaining suspects for their 27× e2e gap, in order: per-op orchestration/readbacks (5-15ms mapAsync floor × their op-granular engine), Tint cold pipeline compile of their era (746-1447s pre-Chrome-141), and co-run/zombie-polluted measurement (see below).

**Bench-integrity discovery (cost us ~1.5h tonight, protocol now hardened): in-flight Metal dispatches SURVIVE the death of the submitting process.** A catastrophic-tier dispatch killed by watchdog/SIGTERM keeps the device at 100% Device Utilization for 20-80 min and silently degrades every subsequent measurement 2-8× (mimics an occupancy knee!). All intermediate "collapsed tier" readings tonight (wg128/256 at 0.03-0.1) were exactly this artifact — clean re-runs put them at roof. Mitigations now in the harness: `run-arm` gates timed passes on `ioreg -r -c IOAccelerator` Device Utilization <10% after taking the lock; NEVER kill a possibly-catastrophic dispatch — size probes to complete (tiny n·k via ec-probe.mjs). Wave-2+ rule: probe every NEW kernel shape with tiny n·k before full-size dispatch; a bad shape is not "slow", it is an hour-long GPU zombie on the user's machine (and plausibly June's too).

**Verdicts:**
1. Composition tax under Tint on M4 = **5-7%** (binv control 0.915 vs composed 0.86-0.88 vs W1d pure-chain roof 0.928). Falloff curves are FLAT across wg 32-256 and across state size 3→9 Fq; the only structure is the saturation ladder (=W1d's) and the binary inline wall.
2. **GO** against the ≥0.6 Gmul/s-equiv composed-bucket-add threshold: **0.865-0.870 measured (both arms) = 1.44× threshold.** Arithmetic: threshold 0.6 = 2× the est. CPU-WASM EC-effective rate (W1d CPU-WASM mul roof ≈0.46 × ~65% EC composition efficiency ≈ 0.3); measured 0.865 = 2.9× that estimate and 1.9× the CPU-WASM pure-mul roof itself — i.e. the GPU bucket-add rate exceeds CPU-WASM's theoretical mul throughput even before CPU pays ITS composition tax. In-browser budget: ~79 M Jacobian mixed-adds/s.
3. Best shape for the real backend: **Jacobian madd-2007-bl guarded, rolled loop, ONE add-class op per iteration, wg 64 (any of 32-256 works), Fe8-packed streamed operands, ≥64K threads live (97% roof; 256K = 100%)**. XYZZ is a proven alternative (+12% point-ops/s at equal Gmul, −1 mul/op, +64B/point residency) — A/B in situ at the W2 commit slot. Guards stay. HARD RULE: never ≥3 add-class bodies inlined per loop body (u2 max, and u2 buys nothing — stay rolled); dbl-class free to 8×.
4. Not NO-GO, so mechanism-of-failure reporting is moot — but for the record the wall we found is a cliff, not a knee: no slimming variant needed anywhere in the shippable envelope (XYZZ/wg-mem spill/2-pass splits all unnecessary — measured shapes already at roof).

Files: suite+probe committed @b33e0a8, results JSONs + utilization gate @20dbbc5 (`wgsl-bench/results/{chrome,node}-default-ec-{safe,risky}.json`). Incidents for orchestrator awareness: playwright cache (`~/Library/Caches/ms-playwright`) was pruned by a co-running campaign mid-bench — run-arm now auto-falls-back to system Chrome (150.0.7871.187, numbers indistinguishable from CfT); a stale bench lock from a crashed run cost 10min (crash-released now); co-running Chrome GPU work (02:45-03:52) polluted early probes — the utilization gate closes this class.

| st7 | HammingWeight claim prep | 0.3 | 0.1 | 0.8 | 0.8 | S |

Cross-trace ranges @2^22 (2 load-contaminated + 1 clean): st0 40.4–45.7, st8 16.7–23.3, st6b 12.5–15.3(16.1), st5 6.7–8.0, st1 5.0–8.1. Scale trend 2^20→2^22: T-linear sumcheck stages GROW (st5+st6b 16.4%→23.3%), st0/st8 shares shrink — the fold front matters MORE at bigger scale. engine::prove − prove ≈ 0.8s @2^22 (tracer emulation `trace` span 0.60s + pad + witness ctor) — the X21 witness-collect choke lives INSIDE st0's one-hot chunk CPU, not pre-prove.

**st0 anatomy (the headline).** Worker CPU @2^22 clean: 352 CPU-s over 39.3s wall = 9.0× (10 workers). Split: `multi_pair_g2_setup_parallel` **231.6 CPU-s (65.7%)**, `stream_process_one_hot_chunks_with` 108.9 (30.9%), `stream_feed_i128_rows_with` 11.9 (3.4%). @2^20: 76.9% / 20.9% / 2.2%. Span len fields: 68 g2-setup multipair calls totaling **345,086 pairs** (42 commit-absorb calls × 8192 rows + st8 reduce tails) → **675 µs·core/pair ≈ 10K Fq muls/pair at measured WASM mul rate — sanity-consistent with Miller+prep+conversion budget. The tier-2 pairing absorb IS the commit stage on WASM.**

**Free CPU fix found (do before/with GPU lanes):** dory-pcs 0.4.0 `multi_pair_g2_setup_parallel` re-prepares the FIXED Γ2 SRS points on every call unless the prepared cache is populated — the `cache` feature IS compiled in (jolt workspace feature union), `init_cache()` exists, but **only jolt-prover-legacy calls it (dory_globals.rs:618); the modular prover never does**. Plus per-point projective→affine conversions (individual inversions, no batching) on both G1 rows and G2. G2-prep+conversion ≈ 25–35% of the 231.6 CPU-s → **~60–80 CPU-s ≈ 6–8s wall ≈ 6–8% whole-prove for ~a one-line init call** (34MB prepared-cache footprint @8192 rows — fine in 4GB). Native profiles pay this too (Metal tree shares the modular path) — check the Metal close-out numbers before assuming their st0 split transfers.

#### D2 — CPU-WASM streaming bandwidth (bench_stream, bare page, fresh pool per thread count, load1 2.9–4.3, median of 7 passes)

Logical GB/s (semantic bytes) and Gmul/s where applicable:

| kernel (shape) | t=1 | t=4 | t=8 | t=10 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| copy_u64 (16B/e, 2^25) | 81.7 | 97.9 | 98.4 | **102.6** |
| sum_u64 (8B/e, 2^26) | 24.1 | 82.0 | 103.7 | **110.1** |
| scale_u64 (16B/e) | 52.6 | 96.3 | 100.0 | **103.5** |
| triad_u64 (24B/e) | 8.9 | 40.3 | 57.7 | **72.0** (≈96 physical w/ write-allocate) |
| fold_fr (96B/out, 1 mul: dst=lo+r·(hi−lo)) | 2.5 / 0.026 | 6.6 / 0.068 | 9.6 / 0.100 | **14.3 GB/s / 0.149 Gmul/s** (2^23=2^24) |
| scale_fr (64B/e, 1 mul) | 2.0 / 0.031 | 5.2 / 0.081 | 7.1 / 0.111 | **10.5 / 0.164** |
| mulchain_fr (dependent ×64) | 0.029 | 0.078 | 0.104 | **0.120 Gmul/s** |

**Boundary verdict (explicit): FOLDS GO GPU.** (1) On raw byte-moving (u64 copy/sum/scale) CPU-WASM SATURATES the unified bus — 102–110 GB/s, ≥ the GPU's 89–97 — so nothing is won by moving pure memcpy-shaped work. (2) But real folds are Fr-arithmetic, and there CPU-WASM is ALU-capped at **0.149–0.164 Gmul/s moving only 10–14 GB/s — 7× short of the bus**. GPU streaming Fr = 0.40 Gmul/s (raw layout) to 0.93 (packed 8×u32, W1d) → **GPU folds win 2.7× (conservative) to 6.2× (packed layout), provided residency (no per-round readbacks)**. W1c's fold_rows gate (§2 row 'fold_rows', §4 row 4) resolves GPU-ward; packed limb layouts remain a design requirement. (3) **The WASM tax is ALL in the ALU, none in memory**: native all-core mont-mul 0.59 Gmul/s vs WASM 0.15–0.16 → **~3.9× field-mul tax** (W1d's "CPU-WASM ≈0.46" was an unmeasured estimate — kill it); streaming tax ≈ 1.0×. GPU-vs-CPU-WASM per-mul edge is really **5.7–6.2×**, not the 2–3× planning number. Corollary: native/Metal-derived profiles UNDERSTATE mul-bound stages on WASM (st0 41% here vs ~32% Metal-native) — the GPU's opening is widest exactly where the WASM tax concentrates: pairing, commit, reduce, and Fr folds.

Pools (wall-equiv): st0-pairing 25.8s, st0-onehot(witness+tier1) 12.1s, st0-i128 1.3s, st8 21.0s (hints 7.0 + reduce/proof 14.0), st6b 14.7s, st5 7.7s, st1–4+6a+7 13.6s, outside-prove 0.8s.

### W3a — dory cache fix (Jul 31 05:55 ET) — prepared-Γ2 cache wired into the modular prover: −7.4% @2^20, −5.9% @2^22, byte-parity confirmed

**Status: COMPLETE.** Jolt branch `webgpu/dory-cache-fix` @ca4c745ba (1 commit on f28761453, worktree `.worktrees/w3a-dory-cache`). Demo worktree `.worktrees/w3a-verify` (branch `w3a/verify` @649a1fc) left **dirty-only, nothing committed**: 5 Cargo.toml path-pin edits (webgpu-jolt-base → w3a-dory-cache, worktree-name churn) + an env-gated `DUMP_PROOF_DIR` proof-bytes dump in test_roundtrip.rs used for the parity gate. Never pushed.

**What was wired where (all in `crates/jolt-dory/src/scheme.rs`, +77/−9):**
- `prime_prepared_cache(setup)` — called at the top of `commit_rows_tier_2` (the single choke point for ALL tier-2 absorbs: scheme commit + 4 streaming finish paths) and before both `dory::prove` entries (`open`, `open_zk`). Lazy absorb-time priming, NOT setup-time: `setup_verifier` runs `setup_prover`, so setup-time priming would charge verifier-only processes the cache footprint; and the demo never calls `setup_prover` at all (setup is DESERIALIZED — the reason the legacy-style wiring can't work here).
- **The footgun that made the tree skip `init_cache` is closed, not inherited.** The old comment was right: dory's `init_cache` only length-checks and `multi_pair_{g1,g2}_setup_*` prefix-match the global cache positionally, so two setup sizes in one process (URS seeds generator values on exact size) silently pair against wrong generators. The primer validates the live cache against `setup.g1_vec[0]` (G1Prepared retains its affine point; G2Prepared doesn't, but g1+g2 are rebuilt atomically from one setup, so the G1 check vouches for both) and `invalidate_cache()`+rebuilds on mismatch. jolt-dory 41/41 tests green, including `commit_open_verify_various_sizes`/`property_based_round_trip` which cycle many setup sizes per process, and native test-roundtrip which switches SRS (sha2 → sha2_chain) mid-process. Concurrent proving under two DIFFERENT setups in one process stays unsupported (global cache) — same as legacy.
- **G1-side batch normalization taken too** (the "if easy" bonus): `normalize_rows` batch-normalizes row commitments before dory's pairing helpers, so its per-point projective→affine conversions hit the `z.is_one()` fast path — one batch inversion per absorb instead of one field inversion per row (345K/prove @2^22, both commit and opening-round-0 sides).

**Parity (gate b): proof bytes IDENTICAL.** Run-to-run determinism confirmed first (two fixed-arm runs byte-equal), then base arm at f28761453: sha256(sha2 proof @2^13) = 127b0545…, sha256(sha2_chain proof @2^19) = f4bbda7c… — equal on both arms, io bytes too. Pure memoization confirmed on the real call pattern (incl. st8's g2_setup tail calls).

**Browser bench (gate d): system Chrome headless, 10T, sha2-chain, 3 runs each, mkdir-lock held, ioreg GPU-util gate 2-3% before each pass, no co-running compiles, all proofs valid-in-browser:**

| scale | W2c anchor | W3a runs | median | delta | kHz |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2^20 | 33.19s | 32.15 / 30.54 / 30.72 | **30.72s** | **−7.4%** | 34.1 |
| 2^22 | 88.44s | 85.42 / 83.21 / 82.25 | **83.21s** | **−5.9%** | **50.4 (0.050 MHz)** |

In W2c's expected −6-8% band. First prove per wasm instance carries the one-time cache build (~+1.5s visible in run 1 @2^20; formerly that prep was re-paid 42×/prove). New CPU-WASM canonical walls for GPU lanes to beat: **30.72s @2^20, 83.21s @2^22** (orchestrator to bless + dashboard).

**Footprint (gate d, memory):** @2^22 peak 2.73/2.73/3.40 GB across runs (W1a baseline 3.19 GB) — ceiling unmoved, ~700 MB headroom, 2^23 still OOM-class (needs ~2×). W2c's "~34 MB" cache estimate was low: theoretical = 8192 G2Prepared × ~100 ell_coeffs × 192 B ≈ **~160 MB** (+0.5 MB G1 side); observed peak deltas are smaller/noisy because the fix also REMOVES ~160 MB of per-absorb transient prep allocations. Native side: not benched rigorously (browser was the target); native sha2_chain @2^19 5.31→4.94s across runs is direction-consistent but single-run noise.

**For later waves:** the remaining st0-pairing CPU is now the true Miller-loop floor (the W2c 675 µs·core/pair budget minus the ~25-35% prep share) — the coop-Miller GPU lane attacks a cleaner target. W2b's seam branch merges trivially against this (one file, additive).

### W2b — seam lane (DONE Jul 31 ~05:40 ET) — webgpu arm + engine + TWO device slots live; every parity gate green

**Status: COMPLETE, all gates green.** Branches (local-only): jolt `webgpu/seam` @c1275b9f5 (4 commits on f28761453; worktree `~/dev/jolt/.worktrees/w2-webgpu-seam`), demo `w2/webgpu-seam` @9066502 (worktree `.worktrees/w2-seam`, path-deps into the jolt worktree, arkworks webgpu/wasm-minimal shared). **What's wired (jolt-kernels, feature `webgpu`, off by default — default build byte-untouched):**
- `webgpu/mod.rs` — `JoltBackend::webgpu()` mirrors `metal()`: `optimized()` + fail-closed engine init + per-slot installs with optimized-twin fallbacks. Gates: `webgpu_gate(kind, work)` off a process-global `WebGpuOptions` (`configure()` from JS on wasm; env fallback native: `JOLT_WEBGPU_DISABLE`/`_MIN_TERMS[_<SLOT>]`/`_HANDOFF_LEN`). DEFAULT_MIN_TERMS 2^16, DEFAULT_HANDOFF_LEN 2^12.
- `webgpu/engine.rs` — device-owning executor, June's skeleton verbatim+fail-closed: native = dedicated thread + private rayon pool + pollster; wasm = SAB job queue + `memory.atomic.notify` + `jolt_webgpu_signal_addr`/`jolt_webgpu_drain` exports drained by the dedicated GPU worker (demo `frontend/public/gpu-worker.js`, `Atomics.waitAsync` loop; spawned by worker.js with {module, memory}, `gpu-ready` handshake BEFORE the blocking `webgpu_warmup()`). Jobs are PHASE-sized (1 encoder/submit, ≤1 readback). **`EngineState.slots: HashMap<u64, Box<dyn Any>>` = the device residency map** — wgpu buffers are !Send on wasm and NEVER cross into slot code; slots carry u64 handles. This is the fold-lane residency surface W2c asked about: buffers persist across rounds/stages until explicitly released.
- `webgpu/wgsl.rs` — generated WGSL off `MontgomeryConstants` (no hand-written limbs): unrolled 16-bit CIOS (W1d roof shape, width stays a codegen param), **`FieldId::{Fr,Fq}` — point kernels emit their field core over the BASE field** (jolt-field gained `MontgomeryConstants for Fq`, ported from the metal branch). G1 point layer = W2a's exact KAT'd shapes (dbl-2009-l, guarded madd-2007-bl, guarded add-2007-bl); the ≥3-add-inline hard rule is documented at the source. Kernels: noop, fr_binop, inc_round (fused bind+eval, compacting), inc_reduce, bucket_madd (wg64, rolled, ONE add-class/iter), bucket_reduce.
- Slots: **commit** (`webgpu/commitment.rs`, `(Fr,DoryScheme)` via Metal's TypeId+transmute install) — one-hot windows bucket on device (indices up = 4B/cycle, points never leave the device; 1 job per column-superchunk = 2 chained dispatches + windows×K×96B readback), increment columns + tier-2 pairing finishes stay on the optimized CPU calls; **inc_claim_reduction** — device rounds (fused bind+eval, 64B eval readback/round) with early host handoff at ≤handoff_len into the SHARED optimized IncKernel (`from_bound_tables` — CUDA's parity-insurance recipe, implemented and tested).
- Harnesses: naga tier = `cargo nextest run -p jolt-kernels --features webgpu` (162 pass; KATs vs host Fr/ark, gate semantics, lockstep round parity, commit parity incl HINTS, tiny-first kernel probes). Tint tier = `cargo nextest run -p jolt-kernels --features webgpu -E 'test(export_wgsl_vectors)' --run-ignored all` then `node crates/jolt-kernels/wgsl-check/check.mjs crates/jolt-kernels/target/wgsl-vectors` (node-webgpu/Dawn; 5/5 green incl a bucket case byte-compared against an ark-validated host mirror of the exact device op order).

**Proof gates (all green):**
- Native (`test-roundtrip` + `JOLT_DUMP_PROOF_DIR`): arm-off vs arm-on-forced (`JOLT_WEBGPU=1 JOLT_WEBGPU_MIN_TERMS=1`, both slots engaged, `[engine] webgpu arm active` printed) → **proof bytes IDENTICAL** on sha2 (2^13) and sha2_chain (2^19); off-vs-off rerun identical ⇒ prover deterministic ⇒ byte gate is the real thing. Deep-handoff variant (HANDOFF_LEN=64) also identical.
- Browser (`gate-webgpu.mjs`, server `PORT=8091 node server.mjs` — 8080 was another campaign's; server.mjs now takes PORT): off/off/on sha2-chain @2^16, minTerms=1 → deterministic ✓, engaged ✓, **SHA-256(proof) identical across arms AND across wasm builds** ✓, all verify ✓.

**Engine facts measured (Chrome 150 headless, M4):** gpu-worker spawn→ready 5-8ms; `webgpu_warmup` (adapter+device+ALL pipelines through the SAB queue) 535ms at 4 pipelines → **1108ms at 6 (the two Fq point kernels ≈ +570ms of Tint compile)** — warmup stays outside any timed window; per-round inc job (submit+64B readback) native ≈ sub-ms, browser not separately timed (parity runs only — no timed benches from this lane; W2a's zombie/ioreg discipline applies when someone benches). wasm build (full demo incl wgpu) 2m16s-2m35s; native engine bring-up ≈ 0.4s.

**Landmines for the next worker:**
1. **Point kernels compute in Fq, not Fr** — the arm's first real bug was generating the G1 field core over Fr (symptom: device buckets all-zero → GT identity commitments). `KernelId::field()` now routes; any new kernel must declare its field.
2. Device Jacobian points are **24 packed words (x‖y‖z, Fe8 each)** — jac_from_words/JAC_WORDS on the Rust side must match (the second bug: 48-word decode read z from the wrong offset).
3. `queue.write_buffer` needs COPY_DST on storage buffers (empty_storage_buffer now includes it).
4. Drop impls that call `engine.run` must check `std::thread::panicking()` (double-panic abort during unwind).
5. server.mjs compressCache: restart after EVERY wasm rebuild (already in W1a's notes; still true).
6. The commit consumer is barrier-style (extract → device per column via par_iter; engine serializes jobs). The optimized arm's extraction/commit PIPELINING (commit_pipelined) is not replicated device-side yet — fine at parity scales, revisit when tuning.
7. wgsl-check needs `node_modules/webgpu` — npm postinstall is blocked by policy; copy from `wgsl-bench/node_modules/webgpu` (done in the worktree, gitignored).

**Deliberately NOT done (per W2c steer):** commit-slot perf tuning (tier-1 is ~12% of whole-prove; pairing absorb is the st0 monster → W4 coop-Miller), XYZZ in-situ A/B (+12% point-ops/s candidate), fold slots (W3 — but their template exists: inc slot's round loop + EngineState residency), tier-2 overlap (Metal W3a shape), 2^18+ browser scale runs (parity gate ran @2^16; nothing blocks bigger).

**Exact repro:** jolt worktree: `cargo nextest run --cargo-quiet -p jolt-kernels --features webgpu` · Tint: export then `node crates/jolt-kernels/wgsl-check/check.mjs crates/jolt-kernels/target/wgsl-vectors` · demo worktree: `cargo run --release --features native --bin test-roundtrip` (env knobs above) · browser: `CARGO_UNSTABLE_BUILD_STD="panic_abort,std" wasm-pack build --release --target web`, restart `PORT=8091 node server.mjs`, `node gate-webgpu.mjs [iters]`.

### W3-T1 mid-flight note (Jul 31 ~08:2x ET) — naga quarantine + wgsl-check fix (parallel lanes read this)
(1) **naga (wgpu-native Metal) MISCOMPILES fq6-class WGSL modules shape-dependently on this toolchain — silent wrong limbs, no error** (e.g. the Fq12-squaring pair zeroes f; adding a dead branch "fixed" one module and not its sibling). ### W3-T2b — st5 PhaseScanner lane (DONE Jul 31 ~13:5x ET) — st5 traced 7.62→3.07s @2^22 (−60%); scans + adopted cycle rounds device-resident, bytes identical everywhere

**Status: COMPLETE, all parity gates green, zero wedges.** Jolt branch `webgpu/t2b-st5` head **bb2232b04** (6 commits on trunk 585362bfb: b6c9f6b54 seam+kernels+slot, 2550c4350 Tint vectors + naga reserved-word fix, 8d3aab92b chunked upload staging, f99ccfa63 2D cycle grids, bb2232b04 vector regen; worktree `~/dev/jolt/.worktrees/w3-t2b`). Demo branch `w3/t2b-verify` @495031e (BENCH_URL/BENCH_WEBGPU knobs on bench-chain/trace-chain; worktree `.worktrees/w3-t2b-verify`, port 8095).

**What landed (spec §T2b, scans + cycle adoption — no descopes):**
- **Seam re-derived against trunk** (Metal donor transplanted structurally, not copied): `init_phase` split into `phase_sums` (scanner-or-CPU) + `assemble_phase` (shared assembly ⇒ byte-identical rounds by construction), `condense_cpu`/`rebuild_u_evals`, flat `bucket_flat`+`PresentTable` replacing per-table Vecs, `new_with_scanner` factory on the prepare front. **Deviation from Metal's contract, deliberate: the scanner OWNS `u_evals` for its lifetime** (device-resident across all 16 phases; Metal condensed the host slice in place through zero-copy wraps) — the kernel's host copy drops at install, `ScanOutcome::Corrupt` rebuilds from intact rows+v_tables (mock-tested 3 phases deep). Cycle side keeps trunk's Pending lifecycle: `CycleTables::Device{pending, pending_bind}` defers each bind into the next fused device round; demotion at ANY point (handoff, decline, failure) is lossless — take_cycle_tables→Dense at the shrunken length, or fall back to the retained host Pending, re-applying the deferred bind (`bind_cycle_tables` shared).
- **Kernel family (7 pipelines, all Fr):** `irr_phase_scan` (fused condense+RAF, interleaved row stripes, per-THREAD partial cell rows in device memory — skew-immune, zero synchronization; deliberately NOT Metal's subgroup-cooperative shape: no subgroup ops/barriers under either compiler), `irr_suffix_scan` (bucket-scheduled, 43-case `suffix_mle` port on u32-pair u64 emulation), generic group-ranged `irr_reduce` (serves RAF cells, per-table suffix cells, AND cycle lanes), `irr_cycle_{pending,mat,fold}` (F=9 product grid, ROLLED factor loop + named lane accumulators p1..p8+p∞ — sidesteps Tint function-space array spills AND naga's big-straight-line hang class; wg32 keeps 9 reduction banks at 9 KiB), test-only `irr_suffix_probe`. Raw-space trick verbatim: `u·scalar` cells accumulate `mont_mul(u, scalar_raw)` on device, host applies one value-space ×R — byte-identical to the CPU deferred accumulator.
- **Phase job = 1 submit, 4 dispatches, ONE ~1.6 MB readback** (reduced cells only); cycle round = 1 submit, 2 dispatches, 288 B lane readback; adopt materializes nothing (pending-eval at full T, materialize fused into the first bind). Per-prove job count ≈ 16 phases + ~11 cycle rounds + take.
- **P2 rows: duplicated T2a's exact contract** (handle 0, 12-word layout, (ptr,len) generation) — merge wave dedupes to one helper; uploads are chunked (8 MB) off borrowed slices per T2a's staging landmine, zero whole-trace staging Vecs (my arm adds ~0 wasm heap; measured identical 3.81 GB arm-on peak as pre-T2b T3).

**Gates (all green, on-device engagement VERIFIED — see incident):** naga suite 167 pass + export (incl. full-round lockstep device-vs-CPU, 2-wg-slice 2D-grid lockstep, decline-at-adopt, Corrupt-rebuild, exhaustive suffix probe); Tint wgsl-check **8/8** (suffix probe 1.1K cases, the real 4-dispatch scan chain w/ ×R-adjusted expectations, F=9 fold chain); native proof bytes IDENTICAL off vs on-forced × handoff {64, default, 10^9} × default-gates @2^13+2^19; browser gate @2^16 deterministic + engaged + SHA-identical + valid. Warmup 13 pipelines ≈ 1.40 s (+~0.3 s vs 6).

| pair | off | on (defaults) | Δ |

- **@2^22 window caveat:** both arms ran under decaying ambient load (W4-R fat-LTO build + searchpartyd; 1-min loadavg 3.5–3.9 at run starts) — pair-internal deltas stand, absolute walls are ~15–25% above clean-window levels (T2a's blessed range 59–73 s off). @2^20 pair is from a clean 11:00-window. **The @2^20 net-NEGATIVE whole-arm is NOT st5** (traced: st5 −0.86, st0 +0.89 commit-slot, st6b +0.30, st1–4 +0.3): the W2b commit slot at default gates costs more than it saves at 2^20 on plain trunk — T4-step-1's known attribution problem, now with a concrete number.

- Wedges: **0 of 11** GPU-arm runs (T1's 1-in-8 SAB missed-wakeup did not fire for me).

**Landmines for successors:**
1. **65535-workgroup-per-dimension limit**: any thread-per-element dispatch at 2^22 with wg32 = 65,536 workgroups = ONE over → Dawn drops the dispatch, downstream reads stale zeros. Symptom was the engine's own stage-5 sumcheck self-check aborting the prove (fail-visible, no bad proof). Audit EVERY per-element kernel at max scale; my fix = 2D grid + `aux1` linearization + a small-slice naga lockstep. Native 2^19 and browser 2^16/2^20 gates all sat under the limit — scale-boundary bugs need a ≥2^22 leg somewhere.
2. **naga reserved words strike again**: `sm`/`meta`… I used `meta` as a WGSL local — naga rejects at PARSE (T2a saw Tint reject it; either compiler can be the strict one) → engine init fails → **every device-gated test silently passes vacuously** (the `let Ok(engine) … else return` skip pattern). After ANY change that can break engine init, verify engagement with `--no-capture | grep skipping`. My first "166/166 green" was partially vacuous for half a session.
3. **Debug-build host suffix oracles panic where release wraps** (`LeftShiftWHelper` `1u32<<32+`, `Pow2`-class `split(6)` underflow at len<6): the prover ships release semantics (= the shader port); test oracles must `catch_unwind`-skip those inputs (kept: native release byte gates pin the wrapping branch on real traces).
4. Cycle kernels bake ra_count=8/F=9 (production one-hot geometry: 16-bit virtual chunks over 128-bit addresses); other shapes decline to CPU at adopt. If the one-hot config ever changes, regenerate IRR_RA_COUNT and the lane lines.
5. st5 remaining levers (not taken): suffix-scan gather sits near the 0.40 raw-stream floor (Fe8-packing the u_evals gather or sorting bucket_flat by row could lift it), NT=4096/2048 thread counts unswept, `claim_columns` + output-claims eq fold still host, first-phase u_evals upload could fuse into the phase-0 scan.
